AND<br>Patient must have been assessed by a physician with expertise in the management of PAH; AND<br>Patient must have WHO Functional Class III PAH or WHO Functional Class IV PAH; AND<br>The treatment must be the sole PBS-subsidised PAH agent for this condition.<br>A prior PAH agent is any of: ambrisentan, bosentan, macitentan, sildenafil, tadalafil, epoprostenol, iloprost, riociguat.<br>Applications for authorisation of initial treatment must be made via the Online PBS Authorities System (real time assessment) or in writing via HPOS form upload or mail.<br>If the application is submitted through HPOS form upload or mail, it must include:<br>(a) details of the proposed prescription; and<br>(b) a completed authority application form relevant to the indication and treatment phase (the latest version is located on the website specified in the Administrative Advice).<br>(1) Confirm that the patient has a diagnosis of pulmonary arterial hypertension (PAH) in line with the following definition:<br>(a) mean pulmonary artery pressure (mPAP) at least 25 mmHg at rest and pulmonary artery wedge pressure (PAWP) no greater than 15 mmHg; or<br>(b) where right heart catheterisation (RHC) cannot be performed on clinical grounds, right ventricular systolic pressure assessed by echocardiography (ECHO) is greater than 40 mmHg, with normal left ventricular function.<br>(2) Confirm that in forming the diagnosis of PAH, the following tests have been conducted:<br>- RHC composite assessment; and<br>- ECHO composite assessment; and<br>- 6 Minute Walk Test (6MWT)<br>Where it is not possible to perform all 3 tests on clinical grounds, the expected test combination, in descending order, is:<br>- RHC plus ECHO composite assessments;<br>- RHC composite assessment plus 6MWT;<br>- RHC composite assessment only.<br>In circumstances where RHC cannot be performed on clinical grounds, the expected test combination, in descending order, is:<br>- ECHO composite assessment plus 6MWT;<br>- ECHO composite assessment only.<br>(3) Document the findings of these tests in the patient's medical records, including, where relevant only, the reason/s:<br>(i) for why fewer than 3 tests are able to be performed on clinical grounds;<br>(ii) why RHC cannot be performed on clinical grounds - confirm this by obtaining a second opinion from another PAH physician or cardiologist with expertise in the management of PAH; document that this has occurred in the patient's medical records.<br>(4) Confirm that the test results are of a recency that the PAH physician making this authority application is satisfied that the diagnosis of PAH is current.<br>(5) Confirm that this authority application is not seeking subsidy for a patient with pulmonary hypertension secondary to interstitial lung disease associated with connective tissue disease, where the total lung capacity is less than 70% of predicted.<br>The test results must not be more than 6 months old at the time of application.<br>Approvals for prescriptions for dose titration will provide sufficient quantity for dose titrations by 0.5 mg increments at 2-week intervals to achieve up to a maximum of 2.5 mg three times daily based on the dosage recommendations for initiation of treatment in the TGA-approved Product Information. |  | C18087 |  | Symptomatic Type I, II or IIIa spinal muscular atrophy (SMA)<br>Initial treatment<br>The condition must have genetic confirmation of 5q homozygous deletion of the survival motor neuron 1 (SMN1) gene; OR<br>The condition must have genetic confirmation of deletion of one copy of the SMN1 gene in addition to a pathogenic/likely pathogenic variant in the remaining single copy of the SMN1 gene; AND<br>Patient must have experienced at least two of the defined signs and symptoms of SMA type I, II or IIIa prior to 3 years of age; AND<br>The treatment must be given concomitantly with best supportive care for this condition; AND<br>The treatment must not be in combination with PBS-subsidised treatment with nusinersen for this condition; AND<br>The treatment must be ceased when invasive permanent assisted ventilation is required in the absence of a potentially reversible cause while being treated with this drug.<br>Must be treated by a specialist medical practitioner experienced in the diagnosis and management of SMA associated with a neuromuscular clinic, or in consultation with a specialist medical practitioner experienced in the diagnosis and management of SMA associated with a neuromuscular clinic.<br>Patient must not have received PBS-subsidised gene therapy.<br>Patient must be 18 years of age or under.<br>Defined signs and symptoms of type I SMA are:<br>i) Onset before 6 months of age; and<br>ii) Failure to meet or regression in ability to perform age-appropriate motor milestones; or<br>iii) Proximal weakness; or<br>iv) Hypotonia; or<br>v) Absence of deep tendon reflexes; or<br>vi) Failure to gain weight appropriate for age; Rolls from side to side on back;<br>2. Child holds head erect for at least 3 seconds unsupported;<br>3. Sitting, but with assistance;<br>4. Sitting without assistance:<br>(a) Child sits up straight with the head erect for at least 10 seconds;<br>(b) Child does not use arms or hands to balance body or support position.<br>5. Hands and knees crawling:<br>(a) Child alternately moves forward or backwards on hands and knees;<br>(b) The stomach does not touch the supporting surface;<br>(c) There are continuous and consecutive movements at least 3 in a row.<br>6. Standing with assistance:<br>(a) Child stands in upright position on both feet, holding onto a stable object (e.g. furniture) with both hands and without leaning on object;<br>(b)The body does not touch the stable object, and the legs support most of the body weight;<br>(c) Child thus stands with assistance for at least 10 seconds.<br>7. Standing alone:<br>(a) Child stands in upright position on both feet (not on the toes) with the back straight;<br>(b) The leg supports 100% of the child's weight;<br>(c) There is no contact with a person or object;<br>(d) Child stands alone for at least 10 seconds.<br>8. Walking with assistance:<br>(a) Child is in an upright position with the back straight;<br>(b) Child makes sideways or forced steps by holding onto a stable object (e.g. furniture) with 1 or both hands;<br>(c) One leg moves forward while the other supports part of the body weight;<br>(d) Child takes at least 5 steps in this manner.<br>9. and<br>(b) a completed authority application form relevant to the indication and treatment phase (the latest version is located on the website specified in the Administrative Advice).<br>(1) Confirm that the patient has a diagnosis of pulmonary arterial hypertension (PAH) in line with the following definition:<br>(a) mean pulmonary artery pressure (mPAP) at least 25 mmHg at rest and pulmonary artery wedge pressure (PAWP) no greater than 15 mmHg; and<br>- ECHO composite assessment; and<br>- 6 Minute Walk Test (6MWT)<br>Where it is not possible to perform all 3 tests on clinical grounds, the expected test combination, in descending order, is:<br>- RHC plus ECHO composite assessments;<br>- RHC composite assessment plus 6MWT;<br>- RHC composite assessment only.<br>In circumstances where RHC cannot be performed on clinical grounds, the expected test combination, in descending order, is:<br>- ECHO composite assessment plus 6MWT;<br>- ECHO composite assessment only.<br>(3) Document the findings of these tests in the patient's medical records, including, where relevant only, the reason/s:<br>(i) for why fewer than 3 tests are able to be performed on clinical grounds;<br>(ii) why RHC cannot be performed on clinical grounds - confirm this by obtaining a second opinion from another PAH physician or cardiologist with expertise in the management of PAH; document that this has occurred in the patient's medical records.<br>(4) Confirm that this authority application is not seeking subsidy for a patient with pulmonary hypertension secondary to interstitial lung disease associated with connective tissue disease, where the total lung capacity is less than 70% of predicted. | Compliance with Written Authority Required procedures |

|  | C18172 |  | Severe active rheumatoid arthritis<br>Initial treatment - Initial 2 (change or recommencement of treatment after a break in biological medicine of less than 24 months)<br>Must be treated by a rheumatologist; OR<br>Must be treated by a clinical immunologist with expertise in the management of rheumatoid arthritis.<br>Patient must have received prior PBS-subsidised treatment with a biological medicine for this condition; OR<br>Patient must have received prior PBS-subsidised treatment with a biological medicine under the paediatric Severe active juvenile idiopathic arthritis/Systemic juvenile idiopathic arthritis indication; It is recommended that an application for the continuing treatment be submitted no later than 4 weeks from the date of completion of the most recent course of treatment. |  | C18180 |  | Severe active juvenile idiopathic arthritis<br>Initial treatment - Initial 1 (new patient)<br>Must be treated by a rheumatologist; OR<br>Must be treated by a clinical immunologist with expertise in the management of rheumatoid arthritis.<br>Patient must have a documented history of severe active juvenile idiopathic arthritis with onset prior to the age of 18 years; and/or (ii) leflunomide at a dose of at least 10 mg daily; and/or (iii) sulfasalazine at a dose of at least 2 g daily; OR<br>Patient must have failed, in the 24 months immediately prior to the date of the application, to achieve an adequate response to a trial of at least 3 months of continuous treatment with a DMARD where 2 of: (i) hydroxychloroquine, (ii) leflunomide, (iii) sulfasalazine, are either contraindicated according to the relevant TGA-approved Product Information or cannot be tolerated at the doses specified above in addition to having a contraindication or intolerance to methotrexate: the remaining tolerated DMARD must be trialled at a minimum dose as mentioned above; All measures must be no more than one month old at the time of initial application.<br>If the above requirement to demonstrate an elevated ESR or CRP cannot be met, the application must state the reasons why this criterion cannot be satisfied.<br>The authority application must be made in writing and must include:<br>(1) details of the proposed prescription; and<br>(2) a completed authority application form relevant to the indication and treatment phase (the latest version is located on the website specified in the Administrative Advice).<br>At the time of authority application, medical practitioners must request the appropriate number of vials of appropriate strength to provide sufficient drug, based on the weight of the patient, for one infusion. A separate authority prescription form must be completed for each strength requested. AND<br>Patient must not receive more than 16 weeks of treatment under this restriction.<br>Patient must be at least 18 years of age.<br>If methotrexate is contraindicated according to the TGA-approved product information or cannot be tolerated at a 20 mg weekly dose, the application must include details of the contraindication or intolerance including severity to methotrexate. The maximum tolerated dose of methotrexate must be documented in the application, if applicable.<br>The application must include details of the DMARDs trialled, their doses and duration of treatment, and all relevant contraindications and/or intolerances including severity.<br>The requirement to trial at least 2 DMARDs for periods of at least 3 months each can be met using single agents sequentially or by using one or more combinations of DMARDs, however the time on treatment must be at least 6 months.<br>If the requirement to trial 6 months of intensive DMARD therapy with at least 2 DMARDs cannot be met because of contraindications and/or intolerances of a severity necessitating permanent treatment withdrawal to all of the DMARDs specified above, details of the contraindication or intolerance including severity and dose for each DMARD must be provided in the authority application.<br>The following criteria indicate failure to achieve an adequate response to DMARD treatment and must be demonstrated in all patients at the time of the initial application:<br>an elevated erythrocyte sedimentation rate (ESR) greater than 25 mm per hour and/or a C-reactive protein (CRP) level greater than 15 mg per L; AND either<br>(a) a total active joint count of at least 20 active (swollen and tender) joints; or<br>(b) at least 4 active joints from the following list of major joints:<br>(i) elbow, wrist, knee and/or ankle (assessed as swollen and tender); and/or<br>(ii) shoulder and/or hip (assessed as pain in passive movement and restriction of passive movement, where pain and limitation of movement are due to active disease and not irreversible damage such as joint destruction or bony overgrowth).<br>The joint count and ESR and/or CRP must be determined at the completion of the 6 month intensive DMARD trial, but prior to ceasing DMARD therapy. All measures must be no more than 4 weeks old at the time of initial application.<br>If the requirement to demonstrate an elevated ESR or CRP cannot be met, the application must state the reasons why this criterion cannot be satisfied. Treatment with prednisolone dosed at 7.5 mg or higher daily (or equivalent) or a parenteral steroid within the past month (intramuscular or intravenous methylprednisolone or equivalent) is an acceptable reason.<br>Where the baseline active joint count is based on total active joints (i.e. more than 20 active joints), response must be determined according to the reduction in the total number of active joints. Where the baseline is determined on total number of major joints, the response must be determined on the total number of major joints. If only an ESR or CRP level is provided with the initial application, the same marker must be used to determine response.<br>At the time of the authority application, medical practitioners should request the appropriate number of vials of appropriate strength to provide sufficient drug, based on the weight of the patient, for a single infusion at a dose of 8 mg per kg. A separate authority prescription form must be completed for each strength requested.<br>Up to a maximum of 3 repeats will be authorised.<br>The authority application must be made in writing and must include:<br>(1) details of the proposed prescription; and<br>(2) a completed authority application form relevant to the indication and treatment phase (the latest version is located on the website specified in the Administrative Advice).<br>An assessment of a patient's response to this initial course of treatment must be conducted following a minimum of 12 weeks of therapy and no later than 4 weeks prior the completion of this course of treatment.<br>Where a response assessment is not conducted within the required timeframe, the patient will be deemed to have failed to respond to treatment with this drug, unless the patient has experienced a serious adverse reaction of a severity resulting in the necessity for permanent withdrawal of treatment.<br>If a patient fails to demonstrate a response to treatment with this drug under this restriction they will not be eligible to receive further PBS-subsidised treatment with this drug for this condition. | Compliance with Written Authority Required procedures |
|  | C18276 |  | Severe active rheumatoid arthritis<br>Initial treatment - Initial 3 (recommencement of treatment after a break in biological medicine of more than 24 months)<br>Must be treated by a rheumatologist; OR<br>Must be treated by a clinical immunologist with expertise in the management of rheumatoid arthritis.<br>Patient must have previously received PBS-subsidised treatment with a biological medicine for this condition; AND<br>Patient must have a break in treatment of 24 months or more from the most recent PBS-subsidised biological medicine for this condition; AND<br>Patient must not have failed to respond to previous PBS-subsidised treatment with this drug for this condition; AND<br>Patient must not have already failed/ceased to respond to PBS-subsidised biological medicine treatment for this condition 5 times; AND<br>The condition must have an elevated erythrocyte sedimentation rate (ESR) greater than 25 mm per hour; OR<br>The condition must have a C-reactive protein (CRP) level greater than 15 mg per L; AND<br>The condition must have either: (a) a total active joint count of at least 20 active (swollen and tender) joints; (b) at least 4 active major joints; AND<br>Patient must not receive more than 16 weeks of treatment under this restriction.<br>Patient must be at least 18 years of age.<br>Major joints are defined as (i) elbow, wrist, knee and/or ankle (assessed as swollen and tender); and/or (ii) shoulder and/or hip (assessed as pain in passive movement and restriction of passive movement, where pain and limitation of movement are due to active disease and not irreversible damage such as joint destruction or bony overgrowth).<br>All measures of joint count and ESR and/or CRP must be no more than 4 weeks old at the time of initial application.<br>If the requirement to demonstrate an elevated ESR or CRP cannot be met, the application must state the reasons why this criterion cannot be satisfied. Treatment with prednisolone dosed at 7.5 mg or higher daily (or equivalent) or a parenteral steroid within the past month (intramuscular or intravenous methylprednisolone or equivalent) is an acceptable reason.<br>Where the baseline active joint count is based on total active joints (i.e. more than 20 active joints), response must be determined according to the reduction in the total number of active joints. Where the baseline is determined on total number of major joints, the response must be determined on the total number of major joints. If only an ESR or CRP level is provided with the initial application, the same marker must be used to determine response.<br>At the time of the authority application, medical practitioners should request the appropriate number of vials of appropriate strength to provide sufficient drug, based on the weight of the patient, for a single infusion at a dose of 8 mg per kg. |  | C18279 |  | Severe active juvenile idiopathic arthritis<br>Continuing treatment<br>Must be treated by a rheumatologist; OR<br>Must be treated by a clinical immunologist with expertise in the management of rheumatoid arthritis.<br>Patient must have received this drug as their most recent course of PBS-subsidised biological medicine treatment for this condition; AND<br>Patient must have demonstrated an adequate response to treatment with this drug; AND<br>Patient must not receive more than 24 weeks of treatment per continuing treatment course authorised under this restriction.<br>Patient must be aged 18 years or older.<br>An adequate response to treatment is defined as:<br>an ESR no greater than 25 mm per hour or a CRP level no greater than 15 mg per L or either marker reduced by at least 20% from baseline;<br>AND either of the following:<br>(a) an active joint count of fewer than 10 active (swollen and tender) joints; or<br>(b) a reduction in the active (swollen and tender) joint count by at least 50% from baseline; or<br>(c) a reduction in the number of the following active joints, from at least 4, by at least 50%:<br>(i) elbow, wrist, knee and/or ankle (assessed as swollen and tender); and/or<br>(ii) shoulder, cervical spine and/or hip (assessed as pain in passive movement and restriction of passive movement, where pain and limitation of movement are due to active disease and not irreversible damage such as joint destruction or bony overgrowth).<br>Where the baseline active joint count is based on total active joints (i.e. more than 20 active joints), response will be determined according to the reduction in the total number of active joints. If only an ESR or CRP level is provided with the initial application, the same marker will be used to determine response.<br>The authority application must be made in writing and must include:<br>(1) details of the proposed prescription; and<br>(2) a completed authority application form relevant to the indication and treatment phase (the latest version is located on the website specified in the Administrative Advice).<br>At the time of authority application, medical practitioners must request the appropriate number of vials of appropriate strength to provide sufficient drug, based on the weight of the patient, for one infusion. [86] Schedule 3, after entry for Zidovudine

insert:

| Zilucoplan | C18128 |  | Treatment refractory generalised myasthenia gravis (gMG)<br>Transitioning from non-PBS to PBS-subsidised treatment - Grandfathering treatment - Treatment refractory gMG patients<br>Patient must have received non-PBS-subsidised treatment with this drug for this PBS indication prior to 1 April 2026; AND<br>Patient must have a diagnosis of MGFA Disease Class II to IV; AND<br>Patient must have a positive serology for acetylcholine receptor (AChR) binding autoantibodies; AND<br>Patient must not be experiencing a myasthenic crisis; AND<br>Patient must not be receiving concomitant treatment with any of the following: (i) another gMG biological agent, (ii) immunoglobulin, (iii) plasma exchange (PLEX), (iv) rituximab for this condition; AND<br>Patient must be receiving concomitant treatment with a non-steroidal immunosuppressant (NS-IST); OR<br>Patient must have had a thymectomy; AND<br>Patient must have had a MG-ADL score of 6 (or higher) and a MGC score of 10 (or higher), despite having undergone 2 of the following 3 remission inducing treatments: (i) NS-IST for a minimum of 12 months, (ii) oral corticosteroids for a minimum of 12 months, (iii) a thymectomy; AND<br>Patient must have demonstrated a clinical improvement based on a decrease in MG-ADL score of at least 2 points from baseline; AND<br>Patient must have demonstrated a clinical improvement based on a decrease in MGC score of at least 3 points from baseline; AND<br>The treatment must provide no more than 6 months of therapy under this restriction.<br>Must be treated by a prescriber who is either: (i) a neurologist; (ii) a clinical immunologist with expertise in the treatment of myasthenia gravis; OR<br>Must be treated by a medical practitioner who has consulted at least one of the above mentioned specialist types, with an agreement reached that the patient should be treated with this pharmaceutical benefit on this occasion.<br>The authority application must be via the Online PBS Authorities System, or in writing and must include:<br>(a) details of remission-inducing treatments [date commencement and duration of drug therapy including drug names and dosages, and/or date of the thymectomy];<br>(b) the baseline MG-ADL and MGC scores assessed after completing the 12 months of remission-inducing treatments (include the date the assessment was conducted);<br>(c) the MG-ADL and MGC scores assessed from the most recent course of treatment demonstrating clinical improvement to treatment (include the date the assessment was conducted).<br>If the application is submitted through HPOS form upload or mail, it must include:<br>(1) details of the proposed prescription; and<br>(2) a completed authority application form relevant to the indication and treatment phase (the latest version is located on the website specified in the Administrative Advice).<br>A retrospective assessment for one of the MGC score or MG-ADL score after completing the 12 months remission inducing treatments can be accepted for grandfathered patients in cases where it was not conducted prior to commencing non-PBS-subsidised treatment for this indication. | Compliance with Authority Required procedures |

AND<br>Patient must not be experiencing a myasthenic crisis; AND<br>Patient must be considered by the treating clinician to have rapidly deteriorating gMG disease in the absence of immediate treatment with a gMG biological agent; AND<br>Patient must not be receiving concomitant treatment with any of the following: (i) another gMG biological agent, (ii) immunoglobulin, (iii) plasma exchange (PLEX), (iv) rituximab for this condition; AND<br>Patient must be receiving concomitant treatment with a non-steroidal immunosuppressant (NS-IST); OR<br>Patient must be commencing treatment with an NS-IST within 2 weeks; OR<br>Patient must have had a thymectomy; AND<br>Patient must be receiving concomitant treatment with an oral corticosteroid; AND<br>Patient must not receive more than 3 months of total treatment with gMG biological agents under this PBS indication ('acute severe gMG'); AND<br>Patient must not have accessed a prior PBS-subsidised gMG biological agent; OR<br>Patient must have developed an intolerance/toxicity to a PBS-subsidised gMG biological agent necessitating a change in treatment.<br>Must be treated by a prescriber who is either: (i) a neurologist; (ii) a clinical immunologist with expertise in the treatment of myasthenia gravis; OR<br>Must be treated by a medical practitioner who has consulted at least one of the above mentioned specialist types, with an agreement reached that the patient should be treated with this pharmaceutical benefit on this occasion.<br>The authority application must be via the Online PBS Authorities System or in writing via HPOS form upload or mail.<br>If the application is submitted through HPOS form upload or mail, it must include the following:<br>(1) details of the proposed prescription; and<br>(2) a completed authority application form relevant to the indication and treatment phase (the latest version is located on the website specified in the Administrative Advice). | Compliance with Authority Required procedures |
|  | C18378 |  | Treatment refractory generalised myasthenia gravis (gMG)<br>Initial treatment - Treatment refractory gMG patients<br>Patient must have a diagnosis of MGFA Disease Class II to IV; AND<br>Patient must have a positive serology for acetylcholine receptor (AChR) binding autoantibodies; AND<br>Patient must not be experiencing a myasthenic crisis; AND<br>Patient must not have received treatment with a gMG biologic within 3 months prior to the first authority application for this indication (i.e. in treatment refractory setting); OR<br>Patient must be considered by the treating clinician to have deteriorating gMG disease during a treatment break with a gMG biological agent; AND<br>Patient must not be receiving concomitant treatment with any of the following: (i) another gMG biological agent, (ii) immunoglobulin, (iii) plasma exchange (PLEX), (iv) rituximab for this condition; AND<br>Patient must be receiving concomitant treatment with a non-steroidal immunosuppressant (NS-IST); OR<br>Patient must have had a thymectomy; AND<br>Patient must have a MG-ADL score of 6 (or higher) and a MGC score of 10 (or higher), despite having undergone 2 of the following 3 remission inducing treatments: (i) NS-IST for a minimum of 12 months, (ii) oral corticosteroids for a minimum of 12 months, (iii) a thymectomy; AND<br>The treatment must provide no more than 6 months of therapy per initial authority application.<br>Must be treated by a prescriber who is either: (i) a neurologist; (ii) a clinical immunologist with expertise in the treatment of myasthenia gravis; OR<br>Must be treated by a medical practitioner who has consulted at least one of the above mentioned specialist types, with an agreement reached that the patient should be treated with this pharmaceutical benefit on this occasion.<br>Patients who are considered to have deteriorating gMG disease while on a treatment break with a gMG biologic may qualify with 9 months of remission inducing treatments (rather than 12 months).<br>The authority application must be via the Online PBS Authorities System, or in writing and must include:<br>(a) details of remission-inducing treatments [date commencement and duration of drug therapy including drug names and dosages, and/or date of the thymectomy];<br>(b) the baseline MG-ADL and MGC scores assessed after completing the 12 months of remission-inducing treatments (include the date the assessments were conducted).<br>If the application is submitted through HPOS form upload or mail, it must include:<br>(1) details of the proposed prescription; and<br>(2) a completed authority application form relevant to the indication and treatment phase (the latest version is located on the website specified in the Administrative Advice). | Compliance with Authority Required procedures |
